<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a deterministic polynomial-time construction of strong thin trees. Given a finite <i>k</i>-edge-connected loopless multigraph on at least one vertex, the algorithm constructs a spanning tree meeting every cut in at most a $`C/k`$ fraction of its edges, for a universal constant <i>C</i>. The running time is polynomial in the binary input length, including when parallel-edge multiplicities are encoded in binary.

- **C1** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-tt-algorithm)): There is an absolute constant $C$ with the following property. Given a finite $k$-edge-connected multigraph $G$ on at least one vertex and an integer $k\ge1$, a deterministic algorithm returns a spanning tree $T$ satisfying $$\|\delta_T(S)\|\le \frac Ck\,\|\delta_G(S)\| \qquad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V(G)) \label{eq:ta-thin}$$ in time polynomial in the total binary input length, whether edges are listed explicitly or multiplicities are encoded in binary. For a one-vertex graph the answer is the empty tree.
  - Explanation: A deterministic construction returns a spanning tree controlling every cut at the inverse-connectivity scale in either explicit or binary-multiplicity input form.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-tg-hierarchy)): There is an absolute integer $h_0$ such that, for every integer $h\ge h_0$, every locally $h$-connected hierarchy of $G$, and every collection $\mathcal M$ of nonroot nodes satisfying $\|O(A)\|\ge \|P(A)\|/h$, there is a matrix $D\succ0$ on $\mathsf E$ with $$\begin{aligned} \ip{D}{Z}&\le\ip{L_G}{Z} &&(Z\in\mathcal N),\label{tg:shortcut}\\ \frac1{\|O(A)\|}\sum_{e\in O(A)}b_e^TD^{-1}b_e &\le h^{-31/40} &&(A\in\mathcal M).\label{tg:hierarchy-resistance} \end{aligned}$$ The cutoff is independent of the graph and the hierarchy depth.
  - Explanation: A locally connected hierarchy admits a positive definite shortcut against the metric test cone, with uniformly small marked average resistances.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-tg-dual)): Let $G$ be connected and $\mathcal M$ a nonempty finite collection of nonroot nodes with nonempty parent boundaries. Let $p_*$ be the infimum, over $D\succ0$ satisfying [tg:shortcut](#tg:shortcut), of the largest marked average resistance. Then $$\label{tg:dual-ratio} p_*\le \sup_{p,U} \frac{\displaystyle\sum_{A\in\mathcal M}\frac1{\|O(A)\|} \left(\sum_{e=uv\in O(A)}a_e\right)^2} {\displaystyle\sum_{e=uv\in E(G)}d(u,v)}, \qquad a_e=\|\ip{u_e}{p_u-p_v}\|.$$ Here $p_v$ are Euclidean points whose squared distances $d(u,v)=\norm{p_u-p_v}_2^2$ satisfy the triangle inequalities, the denominator is positive, and $U$ has orthonormal rows $u_e^T$. The Euclidean dimension may be enlarged.
  - Explanation: The shortcut optimum is bounded by a geometric ratio using squared Euclidean pseudometrics and orthonormal rows.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-tg-profiles)): Every finite metric $(S,d)$ has an isometric realization $x\mapsto c_x$ in a convex polytope $Q$ in a finite-dimensional normed space with the following properties. Distance $\rho_x(q)=\norm{q-c_x}$ is affine on $Q$. There is a common $R>0$ and a point $c_x^-\in Q$ such that $$\label{tg:opposite-radii} \norm{q-c_x}+\norm{q-c_x^-}=R\qquad(q\in Q).$$ From any $q\in Q$ one can reach every radial value in $[0,R]$ by moving toward $c_x$ or $c_x^-$, and the change in radius equals the distance moved. Every straight segment in $Q$ has its norm length, and clamped radial functions are piecewise affine on it.
  - Explanation: Doubled distance profiles realize a finite metric in a convex polytope with affine distances and exact radial motion.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-tg-transfer-assigned)): Let $p_v\in\mathbb R^{d_{\mathrm E}}$ be points for which $d(u,v)=\norm{p_u-p_v}_2^2$ is a pseudometric. Realize $d$ in $Q$ as in [tg:profiles](#tg:profiles). Let $U:\mathbb R^{d_{\mathrm E}}\to\mathbb R^{E(G)}$ be a contraction, change its row signs so $a_e=\ip{u_e}{p_u-p_v}\ge0$, and set $$d_e=d(u,v),\qquad N_A=\frac{(\sum_{e\in O(A)}a_e)^2}{\|O(A)\|},\qquad \mathcal E=\sum_{e\in E(G)}d_e.$$ The following three assertions hold in $Q$.* 1. * Let $T$ be a nonempty set of nodes with $N_A>0$ and $N_A\ge\alpha\sum_{e\in O(A)}d_e$, where $0<\alpha\le1$. Define $$\begin{aligned} J&=1+\lceil\log_2(256/\alpha^2)\rceil,& C_2&=128J^4,&\sigma&=\alpha/C_2,\\ L&=2+\lceil\log_2(1/\sigma)\rceil,& H_0&=1+\lceil\log_2(24/(\rho\sigma^2))\rceil,& C_1&=1024\epsilon^{-2}. \end{aligned}$$ If $\beta>1$, $0<\epsilon<1/3$, $0<\rho<1$, and $\sigma^\epsilon\le(48\beta C_1)^{-1}$, there is a $\rho$-geometric sequence consisting either of $\beta$-compact families or of $\sigma^{1+2\epsilon}$-assigned families, with $$\label{tg:captured-mass} \mathsf M\ge \frac{\sigma^\epsilon}{384\beta C_1C_2LH_0} \sum_{A\in T}N_A.$$* 2. * For $G$ $k$-edge-connected, a $\rho$-geometric sequence of $\beta$-compact families with $\rho\le1/12$ and $\beta\ge36$ satisfies $\mathcal E\ge(k/4)\mathsf M$.* 3. *For a locally $k$-connected hierarchy, selected nodes satisfying $\|O(A)\|\ge k\rho\|P(A)\|$, and a $\rho$-geometric sequence of $(24C_3/k)$-assigned families, one has $$\label{tg:assigned-charge} \mathcal E\ge\frac{kC_4}{96C_3}\mathsf M$$ provided $C_4\ge3$, $\rho\le(6C_4)^{-1}$, and $C_3\ge2((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2)$.
  - Explanation: Compact or assigned geometric families capture boundary mass, and their mass is bounded by graph edge length under the respective connectivity conditions.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-tg-response)): Let $H$ be connected and let $G\subseteq H$ be a spanning subgraph. Let $r$ be a sufficiently large integer, put $h=\lfloor r/2\rfloor$, and suppose $G$ and the subgraphs induced by all parts of a nested sequence of partitions are $h$-edge-connected, with the singleton condition vacuous. If $0<s\le r^{1/4}$, there is $Y\in\mathcal K_H$ such that, at every partition $\mathcal P$ in the sequence, all but at most $r^{-1/3}\|E_G(\mathcal P)\|$ crossing edges satisfy $$\label{tg:response-edge} Y\succeq6s\,b_eb_e^T.$$ Here $E_G(\mathcal P)$ denotes the edges whose endpoints lie in different parts of $\mathcal P$.
  - Explanation: A single matrix responds to almost all crossing edges throughout a nested partition chain with connected induced parts.
- **C7** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-ta-extraction)): There are absolute constants $A$ and $r_0$ such that the following holds. If an explicit multigraph $H$ on at least two vertices contains $r\ge r_0$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, one can deterministically compute a rational $X\in\mathcal K_H$ and at least $$(1-Ar^{-1/16})r$$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, every edge $e$ of which satisfies $b_e^TX^{-1}b_e\le r^{-1/4}$. The running time and output encoding length are polynomial in $\|V(H)\|+\|E(H)\|$.
  - Explanation: Convex continuation constructs a rational shortcut and retains a large packing supported on low-resistance edges.
- **C8** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-ta-continuation)): Under [eq:ta-invariant](#eq:ta-invariant), [eq:ta-target-bound](#eq:ta-target-bound), with $s\le r^{1/4}$, there exist $Y\in\mathcal K_H$ and $y$ satisfying [eq:ta-response-LMI](#eq:ta-response-LMI) and $$\sum_{e\in E_H(\mathcal P)}a_e(y) \ge (Q-grt)(\|\mathcal P\|-1) \qquad\hbox{for every }\mathcal P. \label{eq:ta-strong-target}$$
  - Explanation: The maintained partition invariant admits a continuation response with margin beyond the next target.
- **C9** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-ta-separation)): For nonnegative rational capacities $z$ and rational $q\ge0$, the inequalities $z(E_H(\mathcal P))\ge q(\|\mathcal P\|-1)$ have an exact polynomial-time separation oracle. Consequently the full feasible set defined by [eq:ta-response-LMI](#eq:ta-response-LMI), [eq:ta-conic-representation](#eq:ta-conic-representation), [eq:ta-z-constraints](#eq:ta-z-constraints), [eq:ta-computational-packing](#eq:ta-computational-packing) has such an oracle at rational points.
  - Explanation: Partition capacity constraints admit exact polynomial-time separation using minimum spanning tree optimization.
- **C10** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-ta-bits)): Every continuation step can be computed in polynomial binary time. Its returned variables may be taken on a fixed rational grid whose encoding length depends only on $n+m$, not on the denominators of the old capacities $u_e$.
  - Explanation: A uniform interior margin makes continuation computable in polynomial binary time with denominators independent of old capacity denominators.
- **C11** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-ta-frame)): Suppose $B$ is the union of $q$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, where $r/2\le q\le r$ and $r$ exceeds an absolute constant. There are vectors $z_e\in\mathbb R^{n-1}$, obtained from faithful reduced incidence columns by one common invertible linear map and positive individual scalings, such that $$\sum_{e\in B}z_ez_e^T=I,\qquad \\|z_e\\|^2\le\frac{1+4/r}{q}. \label{eq:ta-frame}$$ They admit rational approximations to Euclidean error $2^{-b}$ in time polynomial in $n+\|B\|+r+b$. An exact specification uses polynomial-bit positive rational edge weights followed by whitening; in particular, every subset has exactly its original incidence rank.
  - Explanation: A tree packing admits an isotropic frame preserving incidence ranks and allowing controlled rational approximation.
- **C12** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-psd)): Every rational Hermitian positive semidefinite matrix $A$ can be written exactly as $$A=\sum_{j=1}^M v_jv_j^*, \qquad v_j\in\mathbb Q(\mathrm i)^n,$$ in polynomial bit time, with $M$ and the total encoding length of the columns polynomial in the encoding length of $A$. If $A$ is real, the columns may be taken real. For $A=0$, the sum may be empty.
  - Explanation: Rational Hermitian positive semidefinite matrices have exact rational rank-one decompositions computed in polynomial bit time.
- **C13** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-ta-signing)): Let $X$ and the union $B$ of $q$ trees be as in [prop:ta-extraction](#prop:ta-extraction), retaining $r/2\le q\le r$ trees, and let $z_e$ be the frame of [lem:ta-frame](#lem:ta-frame). Put $$w_e=(X^{-1/2}b_e,z_e)\qquad(e\in B). \label{eq:ta-stacked}$$ For an absolute constant $C_s$, a deterministic polynomial-time procedure finds $A\subseteq B$ satisfying $$\left\\|\sum_{e\in A}w_ew_e^T -\frac12\sum_{e\in B}w_ew_e^T\right\\|_{\mathrm{op}} \le C_s r^{-1/8}. \label{eq:ta-half-frame}$$ An exact polynomial-time rational PSD test certifies the output.
  - Explanation: A rationally checked signing selects a subset with bounded discrepancy in the stacked resistance and packing frame.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ta-sparsification)): For all sufficiently large integers $r$, from an explicit graph $H$ containing $r$ disjoint spanning trees one can construct a subgraph $H'=(V(H),A)$ and an integer $r'$ such that $$\begin{aligned} &r/4\le r'\le r/2,\qquad H'\text{ contains }r'\text{ disjoint spanning trees}, \label{eq:ta-packing-halving}\\ &\|\delta_{H'}(S)\|\le a_r\|\delta_H(S)\|\quad\text{for every cut}, \qquad a_r=\tfrac12+3C_s r^{-1/8}, \label{eq:ta-cut-halving}\\ &\frac{a_r}{r'/r}\le1+C_1r^{-1/16} \label{eq:ta-ratio-loss} \end{aligned}$$ for an absolute $C_1$. The construction is deterministic and takes polynomial bit time, with the certificate in [lem:ta-signing](#lem:ta-signing).
  - Explanation: One sparsification preserves a substantial tree packing while contracting every cut and controlling the relative loss.
- **C15** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cor-ta-fractional-rounding)): Fix once and for all an integer $B\ge\max\{1,C\}$, where $C$ is the constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:tt-algorithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tt-algorithm">1</a>. Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite undirected graph on $n\ge2$ vertices, given by an explicit list of edge records; in this corollary, loops and parallel records are allowed. Given nonnegative rational numbers $x_e,c_e$ in binary, suppose that $$x(\delta_G(S)):=\sum_{e\in\delta_G(S)}x_e\ge1 \qquad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V).$$ There is a deterministic algorithm, polynomial in the total binary input length, that returns a spanning tree $T\subseteq\{e\in E:x_e>0\}$ satisfying simultaneously $$\begin{aligned} \|\delta_T(S)\|&\le24B\,x(\delta_G(S)) &&(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V), \label{eq:ta-fractional-cuts}\\ c(T):=\sum_{e\in T}c_e&\le24B\sum_{e\in E}c_ex_e. \label{eq:ta-fractional-cost} \end{aligned}$$ The cut inequalities are a promise on the input; no metric assumption on the costs is required.
  - Explanation: Rational fractional cut data can be rounded to a tree in positive support controlling every cut and a nonnegative cost functional simultaneously.
- **C16** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-rational-signing)): Given rational Hermitian matrices $H_1,\ldots,H_N$ of rank at most one, a deterministic algorithm returns signs $\sigma_i\in\{-1,1\}$ such that $$\left\\|\sum_i\sigma_iH_i\right\\| \le40\left\\|\sum_iH_i^2\right\\|^{1/2}.$$ Its running time is polynomial in the total binary encoding length. Consequently, for rational columns $v_i$ with $\sum_i v_iv_i^*=I$, the same algorithm gives discrepancy at most $40\sqrt{\max_i\\|v_i\\|^2}$.
  - Explanation: Rational rank-one Hermitian matrices admit a deterministic discrepancy signing with polynomial binary complexity.

        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"tg:transfer\" label=\"tg:transfer\"></span> Let $p_v\\in\\mathbb R^{d_{\\mathrm E}}$ be points for which $d(u,v)=\\norm{p_u-p_v}_2^2$ is a pseudometric. Realize $d$ in $Q$ as in [tg:profiles](#tg:profiles). Let $U:\\mathbb R^{d_{\\mathrm E}}\\to\\mathbb R^{E(G)}$ be a contraction, change its row signs so $a_e=\\ip{u_e}{p_u-p_v}\\ge0$, and set $$d_e=d(u,v),\\qquad\n N_A=\\frac{(\\sum_{e\\in O(A)}a_e)^2}{|O(A)|},\\qquad\n \\mathcal E=\\sum_{e\\in E(G)}d_e.$$ The following three assertions hold in $Q$.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"tg:transfer-extract\" label=\"tg:transfer-extract\"></span> Let $T$ be a nonempty set of nodes with $N_A>0$ and $N_A\\ge\\alpha\\sum_{e\\in O(A)}d_e$, where $0<\\alpha\\le1$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n     J&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(256/\\alpha^2)\\rceil,&\n     C_2&=128J^4,&\\sigma&=\\alpha/C_2,\\\\\n     L&=2+\\lceil\\log_2(1/\\sigma)\\rceil,&\n     H_0&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(24/(\\rho\\sigma^2))\\rceil,&\n     C_1&=1024\\epsilon^{-2}.\n    \\end{aligned}$$ If $\\beta>1$, $0<\\epsilon<1/3$, $0<\\rho<1$, and $\\sigma^\\epsilon\\le(48\\beta C_1)^{-1}$, there is a $\\rho$-geometric sequence consisting either of $\\beta$-compact families or of $\\sigma^{1+2\\epsilon}$-assigned families, with $$\\label{tg:captured-mass}\n     \\mathsf M\\ge\n     \\frac{\\sigma^\\epsilon}{384\\beta C_1C_2LH_0}\n                      \\sum_{A\\in T}N_A.$$*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"tg:transfer-compact\" label=\"tg:transfer-compact\"></span> For $G$ $k$-edge-connected, a $\\rho$-geometric sequence of $\\beta$-compact families with $\\rho\\le1/12$ and $\\beta\\ge36$ satisfies $\\mathcal E\\ge(k/4)\\mathsf M$.*\n\n3.  *For a locally $k$-connected hierarchy, selected nodes satisfying $|O(A)|\\ge k\\rho|P(A)|$, and a $\\rho$-geometric sequence of $(24C_3/k)$-assigned families, one has $$\\label{tg:assigned-charge}\n     \\mathcal E\\ge\\frac{kC_4}{96C_3}\\mathsf M$$ provided $C_4\\ge3$, $\\rho\\le(6C_4)^{-1}$, and $C_3\\ge2((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2)$.",

          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"tg:transfer\" label=\"tg:transfer\"></span> Let $p_v\\in\\mathbb R^{d_{\\mathrm E}}$ be points for which $d(u,v)=\\norm{p_u-p_v}_2^2$ is a pseudometric. Realize $d$ in $Q$ as in [tg:profiles](#tg:profiles). Let $U:\\mathbb R^{d_{\\mathrm E}}\\to\\mathbb R^{E(G)}$ be a contraction, change its row signs so $a_e=\\ip{u_e}{p_u-p_v}\\ge0$, and set $$d_e=d(u,v),\\qquad\n N_A=\\frac{(\\sum_{e\\in O(A)}a_e)^2}{|O(A)|},\\qquad\n \\mathcal E=\\sum_{e\\in E(G)}d_e.$$ The following three assertions hold in $Q$.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"tg:transfer-extract\" label=\"tg:transfer-extract\"></span> Let $T$ be a nonempty set of nodes with $N_A>0$ and $N_A\\ge\\alpha\\sum_{e\\in O(A)}d_e$, where $0<\\alpha\\le1$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n     J&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(256/\\alpha^2)\\rceil,&\n     C_2&=128J^4,&\\sigma&=\\alpha/C_2,\\\\\n     L&=2+\\lceil\\log_2(1/\\sigma)\\rceil,&\n     H_0&=1+\\lceil\\log_2(24/(\\rho\\sigma^2))\\rceil,&\n     C_1&=1024\\epsilon^{-2}.\n    \\end{aligned}$$ If $\\beta>1$, $0<\\epsilon<1/3$, $0<\\rho<1$, and $\\sigma^\\epsilon\\le(48\\beta C_1)^{-1}$, there is a $\\rho$-geometric sequence consisting either of $\\beta$-compact families or of $\\sigma^{1+2\\epsilon}$-assigned families, with $$\\label{tg:captured-mass}\n     \\mathsf M\\ge\n     \\frac{\\sigma^\\epsilon}{384\\beta C_1C_2LH_0}\n                      \\sum_{A\\in T}N_A.$$*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"tg:transfer-compact\" label=\"tg:transfer-compact\"></span> For $G$ $k$-edge-connected, a $\\rho$-geometric sequence of $\\beta$-compact families with $\\rho\\le1/12$ and $\\beta\\ge36$ satisfies $\\mathcal E\\ge(k/4)\\mathsf M$.*\n\n3.  *For a locally $k$-connected hierarchy, selected nodes satisfying $|O(A)|\\ge k\\rho|P(A)|$, and a $\\rho$-geometric sequence of $(24C_3/k)$-assigned families, one has $$\\label{tg:assigned-charge}\n     \\mathcal E\\ge\\frac{kC_4}{96C_3}\\mathsf M$$ provided $C_4\\ge3$, $\\rho\\le(6C_4)^{-1}$, and $C_3\\ge2((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tg-response",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be connected and let $G\\subseteq H$ be a spanning subgraph. Let $r$ be a sufficiently large integer, put $h=\\lfloor r/2\\rfloor$, and suppose $G$ and the subgraphs induced by all parts of a nested sequence of partitions are $h$-edge-connected, with the singleton condition vacuous. If $0<s\\le r^{1/4}$, there is $Y\\in\\mathcal K_H$ such that, at every partition $\\mathcal P$ in the sequence, all but at most $r^{-1/3}|E_G(\\mathcal P)|$ crossing edges satisfy $$\\label{tg:response-edge}\n Y\\succeq6s\\,b_eb_e^T.$$ Here $E_G(\\mathcal P)$ denotes the edges whose endpoints lie in different parts of $\\mathcal P$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ta-extraction",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $A$ and $r_0$ such that the following holds.         "exact_quote": "Suppose $B$ is the union of $q$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, where $r/2\\le q\\le r$ and $r$ exceeds an absolute constant. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Cut edge multiset** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $S\subseteq V(G)$, let $\delta_G(S)$ be the multiset of edges with one endpoint in $S$ and the other in its complement.
- **Edge connectivity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The graph is $k$-edge-connected if $\|\delta_G(S)\|\ge k$ for every nonempty proper $S$.
- **Thin spanning tree** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A spanning tree $T$ is *$\alpha$-thin* in $G$ if $$\|\delta_T(S)\|\le\alpha\|\delta_G(S)\| \qquad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V(G)).$$
- **Metric test cone** ([source](#tg:section)): Define the closed convex cone $$\label{tg:test-cone} \mathcal N=\left\{Z\succeq0: b_{uw}^TZb_{uw}\le b_{uv}^TZb_{uv}+b_{vw}^TZb_{vw} \quad(u,v,w\in V)\right\}.$$
- **Hierarchy** ([source](#tg:section)): A *hierarchy* is a rooted tree of distinct nonempty subsets of $V$, with root $V$, singleton leaves, and children partitioning their parent into at least two sets.
- **Local hierarchy connectivity** ([source](#tg:section)): The hierarchy is *locally $h$-connected* if every $G[A]$ is $h$-edge-connected, with the singleton condition vacuous.
- **Trace-dual cone** ([source](#tg:section)): Its trace-dual cone is $\mathcal N^*=\{A:\ip{A}{Z}\ge0\text{ for all }Z\in\mathcal N\}$.
- **Resistance capacity ramp** ([source](#pm-h-15)): At a parameter $s>0$ and matrix $X\in\mathcal K_H$, define $$R_e(X)=b_e^TX^{-1}b_e,\qquad p_e=\min\{2,(sR_e(X))^{-1}\},\qquad u_e=\max\{0,p_e-1\}. \label{eq:ta-ramp}$$

| Q10 | Common geometric charging coefficient | = | $\frac{1}{320000}$ | Only the hypotheses and parameter range of the cited statement. |
| Q11 | Strict value-oracle interior slack | ≥ | $8.8$ | Only the hypotheses and parameter range of the cited statement. |
| Q12 | Compressed graph edge count | < | $n^4$ | Only the hypotheses and parameter range of the cited statement. |

          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $h_0$ such that, for every integer $h\\ge h_0$, every locally $h$-connected hierarchy of $G$, and every collection $\\mathcal M$ of nonroot nodes satisfying $|O(A)|\\ge |P(A)|/h$, there is a matrix $D\\succ0$ on $\\mathsf E$ with $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\ip{D}{Z}&\\le\\ip{L_G}{Z} &&(Z\\in\\mathcal N),\\label{tg:shortcut}\\\\\n \\frac1{|O(A)|}\\sum_{e\\in O(A)}b_e^TD^{-1}b_e\n &\\le h^{-31/40} &&(A\\in\\mathcal M).\\label{tg:hierarchy-resistance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The cutoff is independent of the graph and the hierarchy depth.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ta-frame",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $B$ is the union of $q$ edge-disjoint spanning trees, where $r/2\\le q\\le r$ and $r$ exceeds an absolute constant. There are vectors $z_e\\in\\mathbb R^{n-1}$, obtained from faithful reduced incidence columns by one common invertible linear map and positive individual scalings, such that $$\\sum_{e\\in B}z_ez_e^T=I,\\qquad\n                  \\|z_e\\|^2\\le\\frac{1+4/r}{q}.\n \\label{eq:ta-frame}$$ They admit rational approximations to Euclidean error $2^{-b}$ in time polynomial in $n+|B|+r+b$. An exact specification uses polynomial-bit positive rational edge weights followed by whitening; in particular, every subset has exactly its original incidence rank.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.2"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ and the union $B$ of $q$ trees be as in [prop:ta-extraction](#prop:ta-extraction), retaining $r/2\\le q\\le r$ trees, and let $z_e$ be the frame of [lem:ta-frame](#lem:ta-frame). Put $$w_e=(X^{-1/2}b_e,z_e)\\qquad(e\\in B).\n \\label{eq:ta-stacked}$$ For an absolute constant $C_s$, a deterministic polynomial-time procedure finds $A\\subseteq B$ satisfying $$\\left\\|\\sum_{e\\in A}w_ew_e^T\n                    -\\frac12\\sum_{e\\in B}w_ew_e^T\\right\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\n                          \\le C_s r^{-1/8}.\n \\label{eq:ta-half-frame}$$ An exact polynomial-time rational PSD test certifies the output.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ta-sparsification",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large integers $r$, from an explicit graph $H$ containing $r$ disjoint spanning trees one can construct a subgraph $H'=(V(H),A)$ and an integer $r'$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n &r/4\\le r'\\le r/2,\\qquad H'\\text{ contains }r'\\text{ disjoint spanning trees},\n          \\label{eq:ta-packing-halving}\\\\\n &|\\delta_{H'}(S)|\\le a_r|\\delta_H(S)|\\quad\\text{for every cut},\n       \\qquad a_r=\\tfrac12+3C_s r^{-1/8},\n          \\label{eq:ta-cut-halving}\\\\\n &\\frac{a_r}{r'/r}\\le1+C_1r^{-1/16}\n          \\label{eq:ta-ratio-loss}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for an absolute $C_1$. The construction is deterministic and takes polynomial bit time, with the certificate in [lem:ta-signing](#lem:ta-signing).",
          "source_id": "PM-174.2"

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-ta-fractional-rounding",
          "exact_quote": "Fix once and for all an integer $B\\ge\\max\\{1,C\\}$, where $C$ is the constant in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:tt-algorithm\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:tt-algorithm\">1</a>. Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite undirected graph on $n\\ge2$ vertices, given by an explicit list of edge records; in this corollary, loops and parallel records are allowed. Given nonnegative rational numbers $x_e,c_e$ in binary, suppose that $$x(\\delta_G(S)):=\\sum_{e\\in\\delta_G(S)}x_e\\ge1\n       \\qquad(\\varnothing\\ne S\\subsetneq V).$$ There is a deterministic algorithm, polynomial in the total binary input length, that returns a spanning tree $T\\subseteq\\{e\\in E:x_e>0\\}$ satisfying simultaneously $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\delta_T(S)|&\\le24B\\,x(\\delta_G(S))\n       &&(\\varnothing\\ne S\\subsetneq V),\n       \\label{eq:ta-fractional-cuts}\\\\\n c(T):=\\sum_{e\\in T}c_e&\\le24B\\sum_{e\\in E}c_ex_e.\n       \\label{eq:ta-fractional-cost}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The cut inequalities are a promise on the input; no metric assumption on the costs is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-174.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-tg-hierarchy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tg-transfer-assigned-2): Conic geometric duality and bag charging -> Positive definite metric-cone shortcut
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-tg-response) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tg-response): Dyadic assembly over nested partitions -> Simultaneous response matrix
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-ta-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-continuation): Partition uncrossing and separation -> Feasible response with partition slack
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-ta-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-separation): Minimum spanning tree separation and rational optimization -> Exact separator for packing constraints
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-ta-bits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-bits): Ellipsoid feasibility with a uniform interior ball and dyadic grid rounding -> Polynomial-bit response computation
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-ta-extraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-bits-2): Iterated resistance capacity continuation and graphic matroid union -> Rational shortcut and low-resistance packing
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-ta-frame) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-frame): Log-determinant descent and faithful incidence whitening -> Isotropic rank-preserving frame
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-psd) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-psd): Pivoted rational elimination and base expansion of pivots -> Exact rational rank-one decomposition
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-rational-signing) / [proof](#pm-h-27): Rational potential descent with exact matrix tests -> Deterministic signing
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-ta-signing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-signing): Stacked vector approximation, identity padding and checked signing -> Certified discrepancy-bounded subset
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-ta-sparsification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-sparsification): Matrix cut control and projected incidence-rank packing -> Smaller graph retaining packing and all-cut control
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-thm-tt-algorithm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ta-sparsification-2): Iterated sparsification and binary multiplicity compression -> Thin spanning tree
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-cor-ta-fractional-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-ta-fractional-rounding): Rational pair compression, repeated tree deletion and least-cost selection -> Simultaneous thin and cheap tree

Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite undirected graph on $n\\ge2$ vertices, given by an explicit list of edge records; in this corollary, loops and parallel records are allowed. Given nonnegative rational numbers $x_e,c_e$ in binary, suppose that $$x(\\delta_G(S)):=\\sum_{e\\in\\delta_G(S)}x_e\\ge1\n       \\qquad(\\varnothing\\ne S\\subsetneq V).$$ There is a deterministic algorithm, polynomial in the total binary input length, that returns a spanning tree $T\\subseteq\\{e\\in E:x_e>0\\}$ satisfying simultaneously $$\\begin{aligned}\n |\\delta_T(S)|&\\le24B\\,x(\\delta_G(S))\n       &&(\\varnothing\\ne S\\subsetneq V),\n       \\label{eq:ta-fractional-cuts}\\\\\n c(T):=\\sum_{e\\in T}c_e&\\le24B\\sum_{e\\in E}c_ex_e.\n       \\label{eq:ta-fractional-cost}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The cut inequalities are a promise on the input; no metric assumption on the costs is required.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-thin**: The deterministic construction returns a spanning tree with the all-cut inverse-connectivity guarantee in either binary input representation.
- **I-compress**: Truncation and integer scaling preserve a polynomial explicit graph with the stated lower and upper cut bounds; supported tree edges can be lifted to genuine original edges.
- **I-cost**: Fractional cut rounding simultaneously bounds cut loads and the nonnegative cost of a tree in positive support.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-directed**: Apply the thin-tree guarantee directly to a directed graph. -> Directed cut and orientation requirements are not the undirected multigraph conclusion.
- **A-expand**: Expand binary multiplicities into all labelled copies before compression. -> The expanded graph may be exponential in input length.
- **A-oracle**: Extend the bit-complexity conclusion to arbitrary real-oracle inputs. -> The promised rational encoding and exact comparisons are unavailable.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The binary-multiplicity reduction is a finite constructive subroutine. This implementation checks compression and its cut bounds; it does not implement the full thin-tree construction.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of cases {n,k,c}; c lists unordered pairs lexicographically.
# PRECONDITIONS: integers 2<=n<=4, k>=1, c>=0, all nontrivial cuts have capacity >=k.
# OUTPUTS: per case compressed multiplicities, or {error: invalid} outside the contract.
# TIME: O(2^n n^2) exact operations per case, including promise checking.
# SPACE: O(n^2) integers per case, excluding input and output batches.
# ARITHMETIC: exact FLINT fmpz integers; no edge-copy expansion or floating point.
# FAILURE: invalid cases return an error object; malformed JSON terminates unsuccessfully.
import json,sys
from itertools import combinations
from flint import fmpz

def compress(case):
    n,k,c=case.get('n'),case.get('k'),case.get('c')
    if type(n) is not int or not 2<=n<=4 or type(k) is not int or k<1:
        return {'error':'invalid'}
    pairs=list(combinations(range(n),2))
    if not isinstance(c,list) or len(c)!=len(pairs) or any(type(x) is not int or x<0 for x in c):
        return {'error':'invalid'}
    for mask in range(1,(1<<n)-1):
        load=sum(c[i] for i,(u,v) in enumerate(pairs) if ((mask>>u)&1)!=((mask>>v)&1))
        if load<k:
            return {'error':'invalid'}
    return [int((2*fmpz(n)**2*min(fmpz(x),fmpz(k)))//fmpz(k)) for x in c]

print(json.dumps([compress(case) for case in json.load(sys.stdin)],separators=(',',':')))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Graph-processing software can use the deterministic spanning-tree construction when its input satisfies the finite undirected multigraph and edge-connectivity promises. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no game mechanics, simulation model or sampling guarantee is established for the finite multigraph construction. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Exact graph and matrix verification tools can adapt the rational packing and positive-semidefinite certificates used by the construction. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper states deterministic polynomial-bit constructions of thin spanning trees, including binary multiplicities, using packing-preserving sparsification and rational signing. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: edge lists and binary multiplicities are input representations; the paper states no standalone data-structure query or update guarantee. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the polynomial-bit algorithm specifies mathematical operations without an accelerator implementation or hardware execution guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: rank-one matrix signing controls discrepancy; the paper supplies no cryptographic authentication or security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: metric-cone optimization and matrix discrepancy do not establish a learning model, training procedure or prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The paper supplies exact separation for partition packing constraints and simultaneous cost-and-cut rounding for nonnegative rational fractional inputs with the all-cut promise. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper gives a deterministic graph construction, not a distributed coordination protocol or a fault model. |
| other (finite metric geometry) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Finite metric geometry applies through the doubled distance-profile realization: affine radii and exact radial motion support the geometric charging argument. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-model** (hypothesis): The main thin-tree guarantee requires finite undirected loopless multigraphs, with parallel copies counted separately. Blocked use: ['Applying the main graph guarantee directly to directed graphs or counting parallel copies as a single edge.']
- **L-connectivity** (hypothesis): The input connectivity promise concerns every nonempty proper vertex subset; a disconnected input does not satisfy the main guarantee. Blocked use: ['Using an unchecked connectivity parameter to claim inverse-connectivity thinness.']
- **L-singleton** (edge_case): The single-vertex input returns the empty tree before any packing or matrix computation. Blocked use: ['Requiring a nonempty tree or invoking a centered inverse on the single-vertex case.']
- **L-large-packing** (hypothesis): Constructive shortcut extraction requires an explicit multigraph with a sufficiently large supplied spanning-tree packing. Blocked use: ['Invoking the extraction step on an arbitrary sparse graph without its packing hypothesis.']
- **L-fractional-model** (hypothesis): The fractional-rounding corollary allows loops and parallel records in its explicit undirected graph input; this broader input convention belongs to that corollary. Blocked use: ['Rejecting the fractional-rounding input merely because it contains loops, or extending the main theorem to a different graph model without its reduction.']
- **L-fractional-promise** (hypothesis): Fractional rounding assumes its cut inequalities and nonnegative rational weights and costs; no metric assumption on costs is needed. Blocked use: ['Claiming the rounding guarantee for negative costs or weights, or for inputs violating the fractional cut promise.']
- **L-real-oracle** (formal_scope): The binary-time guarantee for fractional rounding concerns rationally encoded data and does not cover arbitrary real-oracle inputs. Blocked use: ['Transferring polynomial-bit guarantees to arbitrary real-number oracle representations.']
- **L-pseudometric** (edge_case): Identifying coincident images in the profile geometry preserves all graph labels and does not contract graph vertices or edge copies. Blocked use: ['Contracting the graph because distinct vertex images have zero distance.']
- **L-lifting** (edge_case): Auxiliary multiplicities may exceed original multiplicities; lifting is justified for the returned tree because it uses at most one edge of each parallel pair. Blocked use: ['Lifting an arbitrary auxiliary edge subset while claiming that its multiplicities fit the original graph.']
- **L-existence-shortcut** (nonconstructive): The companion fixed-point construction alone does not provide the finite search bound required by the algorithm; the paper supplies additional computational steps. Blocked use: ['Treating compactness or a fixed point alone as an executable shortcut construction.']
- **L-atsp** (approximation): The rounding consequence supplies simultaneous cut and cost control on undirected support; it makes no new approximation-order claim for asymmetric traveling salesman. Blocked use: ['Presenting the rounding result as a newly improved approximation order for asymmetric traveling salesman.']
- **L-signing** (hypothesis): The rational matrix-signing guarantee requires Hermitian inputs of rank at most one; signing denotes discrepancy control rather than authentication. Blocked use: ['Applying the stated signing theorem to arbitrary higher-rank matrices or using it as a cryptographic signature guarantee.']
- **L-practical-constants** (unknown): A numerical universal thinness constant and practical runtime bound are ABSENT from the main theorem; its guarantee is an absolute constant and polynomial binary time. Blocked use: ['Claiming a calibrated thinness constant or a measured production runtime from the theorem statement.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 174 (`lean/docs/174.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The strong thin tree conjecture](../../preprints/The-strong-thin-tree-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [A polynomial-time construction of strong thin trees](../../preprints/A-polynomial-time-construction-of-strong-thin-trees-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The strong thin-tree conjecture asks for spanning trees that cross every cut sparsely relative to the original graph. The formalized result gives one absolute constant $C>0$ such that every finite loopless $k$-edge-connected multigraph with at least two vertices has a spanning tree crossing each nontrivial cut at most $(C/k)$ times the original cut size. Parallel edges remain distinct. No numerical value of $C$ or construction algorithm is asserted.
>
> The strong thin-tree problem asks for a spanning tree that crosses every cut sparsely relative to the original graph. The formalization gives one deterministic polynomial-time algorithm and an absolute constant $C>0$ such that a finite $k$-edge-connected loopless multigraph yields a spanning tree using at most a $C/k$ fraction of the edges of every cut. The input may encode parallel-edge multiplicities in binary, and the running time is polynomial in that binary input length.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Deterministic polynomial-time construction of strong thin trees. -/

namespace StrongThinTree

structure MultiGraph (n m : ℕ) where
  left : Fin m → Fin n
  right : Fin m → Fin n
  loopless : ∀ e, left e ≠ right e

namespace MultiGraph

def cut {n m : ℕ} (G : MultiGraph n m) (T : Finset (Fin m))
    (S : Finset (Fin n)) : Finset (Fin m) :=
  T.filter fun e => (G.left e ∈ S ∧ G.right e ∉ S) ∨
    (G.right e ∈ S ∧ G.left e ∉ S)

def Connected {n m : ℕ} (G : MultiGraph n m) (T : Finset (Fin m)) : Prop :=
  ∀ S : Finset (Fin n), S.Nonempty → S ≠ Finset.univ → (G.cut T S).Nonempty

def SpanningTree {n m : ℕ} (G : MultiGraph n m) (T : Finset (Fin m)) : Prop :=
  G.Connected T ∧ ∀ e ∈ T, ¬ G.Connected (T.erase e)

def EdgeConnected {n m : ℕ} (G : MultiGraph n m) (k : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ S : Finset (Fin n), S.Nonempty → S ≠ Finset.univ →
    k ≤ (G.cut Finset.univ S).card

end MultiGraph
end StrongThinTree

namespace CurrentKS

def frame : List Bool → List Bool
  | [] => [true]
  | b :: bs => false :: b :: frame bs

def encodeNat (n : ℕ) : List Bool := frame (Computability.encodeNat n)

inductive StackAction where
  | stay
  | pop
  | push (bit : Bool)
  deriving DecidableEq

def StackAction.apply : StackAction → List Bool → List Bool
  | .stay, xs => xs
  | .pop, xs => xs.tail
  | .push b, xs => b :: xs

structure Instruction (k q : ℕ) where
  next : Option (Fin q)
  actions : Fin k → StackAction

def Instruction.halt (k q : ℕ) : Instruction k q := ⟨none, fun _ => .stay⟩

structure Machine where
  stackCount : ℕ
  states : ℕ
  initial : Fin states
  inputStack : Fin stackCount
  outputStack : Fin stackCount
  table : List (Instruction stackCount states)

structure Config (k q : ℕ) where
  state : Option (Fin q)
  «stacks» : Fin k → List Bool

def topCode : List Bool → ℕ
  | [] => 0
  | false :: _ => 1
  | true :: _ => 2

def headsCode : (k : ℕ) → (Fin k → List Bool) → ℕ
  | 0, _ => 0
  | k + 1, s => topCode (s 0) + 3 * headsCode k (fun j => s j.succ)

def Machine.step (M : Machine) (c : Config M.stackCount M.states) :
    Config M.stackCount M.states :=
  match c.state with
  | none => c
  | some q =>
    let instruction := M.table.getD
      (3 ^ M.stackCount * q.val + headsCode M.stackCount c.stacks)
      (Instruction.halt M.stackCount M.states)
    ⟨instruction.next, fun j => (instruction.actions j).apply (c.stacks j)⟩

def Machine.init (M : Machine) (input : List Bool) : Config M.stackCount M.states :=
  ⟨some M.initial, fun j => if j = M.inputStack then input else []⟩

def Machine.run (M : Machine) (input : List Bool) (fuel : ℕ) :
    Config M.stackCount M.states := (M.step^[fuel]) (M.init input)

end CurrentKS

namespace AlgorithmicThinTrees
open StrongThinTree

structure ExplicitInput where
  n : ℕ
  m : ℕ
  k : ℕ
  graph : MultiGraph n m

structure BinaryInput where
  n : ℕ
  p : ℕ
  k : ℕ
  left : Fin p → Fin n
  right : Fin p → Fin n
  ordered : ∀ i, left i < right i
  distinct : Function.Injective (fun i => (left i, right i))
  multiplicity : Fin p → ℕ

abbrev BinaryInput.Edge (x : BinaryInput) := Σ i : Fin x.p, Fin (x.multiplicity i)

noncomputable def BinaryInput.edgeEquiv (x : BinaryInput) :
    x.Edge ≃ Fin (Fintype.card x.Edge) := Fintype.equivFin x.Edge

noncomputable def BinaryInput.graph (x : BinaryInput) :
    MultiGraph x.n (Fintype.card x.Edge) where
  left e := x.left (x.edgeEquiv.symm e).1
  right e := x.right (x.edgeEquiv.symm e).1
  loopless e := ne_of_lt (x.ordered (x.edgeEquiv.symm e).1)

inductive Input where
  | explicit (x : ExplicitInput)
  | binary (x : BinaryInput)

def ExplicitInput.encode (x : ExplicitInput) : List Bool :=
  CurrentKS.encodeNat x.n ++ CurrentKS.encodeNat x.m ++ CurrentKS.encodeNat x.k ++
    (List.ofFn fun i : Fin x.m =>
      CurrentKS.encodeNat (x.graph.left i).val ++
      CurrentKS.encodeNat (x.graph.right i).val).flatten

def BinaryInput.encode (x : BinaryInput) : List Bool :=
  CurrentKS.encodeNat x.n ++ CurrentKS.encodeNat x.p ++ CurrentKS.encodeNat x.k ++
    (List.ofFn fun i : Fin x.p =>
      CurrentKS.encodeNat (x.left i).val ++ CurrentKS.encodeNat (x.right i).val ++
      CurrentKS.encodeNat (x.multiplicity i)).flatten

def Input.encode : Input → List Bool
  | .explicit x => false :: x.encode
  | .binary x => true :: x.encode

def Input.length (x : Input) : ℕ := x.encode.length

def Input.n : Input → ℕ
  | .explicit x => x.n
  | .binary x => x.n

def Valid : Input → Prop
  | .explicit x => 1 ≤ x.n ∧ 1 ≤ x.k ∧ x.graph.EdgeConnected x.k
  | .binary x => 1 ≤ x.n ∧ 1 ≤ x.k ∧ x.graph.EdgeConnected x.k

def ThinTree {n m : ℕ} (C : ℝ) (k : ℕ) (G : MultiGraph n m)
    (T : Finset (Fin m)) : Prop :=
  G.SpanningTree T ∧ ∀ S : Finset (Fin n), S.Nonempty → S ≠ Finset.univ →
    ((G.cut T S).card : ℝ) ≤ C / (k : ℝ) * (G.cut Finset.univ S).card

def ExplicitInput.encodeEdges (x : ExplicitInput) (edges : List (Fin x.m)) : List Bool :=
  edges.flatMap (fun e => CurrentKS.encodeNat e.val)

def BinaryInput.encodeEdges (x : BinaryInput) (edges : List x.Edge) : List Bool :=
  edges.flatMap (fun e => CurrentKS.encodeNat e.1.val ++ CurrentKS.encodeNat e.2.val)

noncomputable def BinaryInput.selected (x : BinaryInput) (edges : List x.Edge) :
    Finset (Fin (Fintype.card x.Edge)) := edges.toFinset.map x.edgeEquiv.toEmbedding

def TreeOutput (C : ℝ) : Input → List Bool → Prop
  | .explicit x, bits => ∃ edges : List (Fin x.m), edges.Nodup ∧
      bits = x.encodeEdges edges ∧ ThinTree C x.k x.graph edges.toFinset
  | .binary x, bits => ∃ edges : List x.Edge, edges.Nodup ∧
      bits = x.encodeEdges edges ∧ ThinTree C x.k x.graph (x.selected edges)

def GoodOutput (C : ℝ) (x : Input) (bits : List Bool) : Prop :=
  TreeOutput C x bits ∧ (x.n = 1 → bits = [])

def AlgorithmicStrongThinTrees : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧ ∃ M : CurrentKS.Machine, ∃ a degree : ℕ, 0 < a ∧
    ∀ x : Input, Valid x →
      let result := M.run x.encode (a * (x.length + 1) ^ degree)
      result.state = none ∧ GoodOutput C x (result.stacks M.outputStack)

end AlgorithmicThinTrees

namespace AlgorithmicThinTrees

theorem algorithmic_strong_thin_trees : AlgorithmicStrongThinTrees := by
  sorry

end AlgorithmicThinTrees

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a deterministic polynomial-time construction of strong thin trees. Given a finite <i>k</i>-edge-connected loopless multigraph on at least one vertex, the algorithm constructs a spanning tree meeting every cut in at most a $`C/k`$ fraction of its edges, for a universal constant <i>C</i>. The running time is polynomial in the binary input length, including when parallel-edge multiplicities are encoded in binary.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A spanning tree retains connectivity with only $n-1$ edges. A thin spanning tree must also use a small fraction of every cut, with the same tree serving all cuts simultaneously. We give a deterministic polynomial-bit construction at the inverse-connectivity scale.

Throughout, graphs are finite undirected loopless multigraphs, and every parallel copy counts separately. For $S\subseteq V(G)$, let $\delta_G(S)$ be the multiset of edges with one endpoint in $S$ and the other in its complement. The graph is $k$-edge-connected if $|\delta_G(S)|\ge k$ for every nonempty proper $S$. A spanning tree $T$ is *$\alpha$-thin* in $G$ if $$|\delta_T(S)|\le\alpha|\delta_G(S)|
 \qquad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V(G)).$$ We consider two input representations: an explicit list of labelled edge copies, or a list of endpoint pairs with nonnegative integer multiplicities written in binary. In the second representation, the number of edge copies may be exponential in the input length.

<a id="stmt-thm-tt-algorithm"></a>
<div id="thm:tt-algorithm" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Algorithmic strong thin trees). *There is an absolute constant $C$ with the following property. Given a finite $k$-edge-connected multigraph $G$ on at least one vertex and an integer $k\ge1$, a deterministic algorithm returns a spanning tree $T$ satisfying <a id="eq:ta-thin"></a>$$|\delta_T(S)|\le \frac Ck\,|\delta_G(S)|
       \qquad(\varnothing\ne S\subsetneq V(G))
 \label{eq:ta-thin}$$ in time polynomial in the total binary input length, whether edges are listed explicitly or multiplicities are encoded in binary. For a one-vertex graph the answer is the empty tree.*

</div>

The universal $C/k$ bound is the strong thin-tree conjecture. The companion article *The strong thin tree conjecture* (OpenAI 2026) proves the existence statement and supplies the complete hierarchy geometry used here. Theorem <a href="#thm:tt-algorithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:tt-algorithm">1</a> gives the positive algorithmic resolution in both finite input models. Its order in $k$ is necessary: for two vertices joined by $k$ parallel edges, the unique cut contains exactly one tree edge.

The construction also gives simultaneous cost-and-cut rounding. Given nonnegative rational edge weights whose mass on every cut is at least one, and any nonnegative rational edge costs, we find a tree in the positive support with every cut load and its total cost bounded by constant multiples of the corresponding fractional quantities (Corollary <a href="#cor:ta-fractional-rounding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:ta-fractional-rounding">15</a>). No metric assumption on the costs is needed.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and prior work

Goddyn’s qualitative conjecture asks whether every positive thinness bound follows from sufficiently large edge connectivity, independently of graph order (Goddyn 2004, Problem 4) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Goddyn2004](#ref-Goddyn2004)]. The strong form specifies the rate $C/k$; the distinction is made explicitly in (Anari and Oveis Gharan 2015, Conjecture 1.3 and the following discussion) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[AnariOveisGharan2015](#ref-AnariOveisGharan2015)]. One source of algorithmic interest is the asymmetric traveling salesman problem, which asks for a minimum-cost tour through the vertices of a directed metric. Asadpour, Goemans, Mądry, Oveis Gharan, and Saberi used maximum-entropy spanning-tree distributions to obtain an $O(\log n/\log\log n)$ approximation (Asadpour et al. 2017) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[AsadpourEtAl2017](#ref-AsadpourEtAl2017)]. Their rounding method also gives the constructive $O(\log n/(k\log\log n))$ thin-tree scale; see (Anari and Oveis Gharan 2015, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[AnariOveisGharan2015](#ref-AnariOveisGharan2015)].

For planar graphs, and more generally on each fixed orientable surface, Oveis Gharan and Saberi obtained constructive inverse-connectivity bounds (Oveis Gharan and Saberi 2011, Theorem 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[OveisGharanSaberi2011](#ref-OveisGharanSaberi2011)]. For general multigraphs, Anari and Oveis Gharan introduced effective-resistance-reducing flows and locally connected hierarchies, and proved the existence of $\operatorname{poly}(\log\log n)/k$-thin trees (Anari and Oveis Gharan 2015, Corollary 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[AnariOveisGharan2015](#ref-AnariOveisGharan2015)]. Their geometric method is the principal foundation of the hierarchy argument in the companion and its extension below. The existence theorem does not itself give a polynomial-time construction of those trees.

Recent work has obtained inverse-connectivity bounds for structured families of cuts. Klein and Olver treat every prescribed laminar family (Klein and Olver 2023) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KleinOlver2023](#ref-KleinOlver2023)]. Klein, Olver, and Yeoh construct a tree crossing each cut of size less than $(1+1/40)k$ at most $88$ times (Klein, Olver, and Yeoh 2026, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[KleinOlverYeoh2026](#ref-KleinOlverYeoh2026)]. Our conclusion controls all cuts of an arbitrary multigraph. Thin trees remain an independent structural problem: constant-factor approximation for asymmetric traveling salesman was already established by Svensson, Tarnawski, and Végh and subsequently improved by Traub and Vygen (Svensson, Tarnawski, and Végh 2020; Traub and Vygen 2022) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[SvenssonTarnawskiVegh2020](#ref-SvenssonTarnawskiVegh2020), [TraubVygen2022](#ref-TraubVygen2022)].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### From the geometric existence proof to an algorithm

High edge connectivity supplies many edge-disjoint spanning trees by the Nash–Williams–Tutte theorem (Nash-Williams 1961; Tutte 1961) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[NashWilliams1961](#ref-NashWilliams1961), [Tutte1961](#ref-Tutte1961)]. A packing bounds the average tree load on each fixed cut, but does not identify one tree that is small on every cut. The companion retains a large packing through successive sparsifications, reducing cut sizes and packing size by almost the same factor. Two vector representations allow the same edge subset to preserve cut control and enough rank information to certify all partition inequalities for tree packing. Iterated matrix halving with a bounded product of losses was used by Harvey and Olver to obtain spectrally thin trees under an effective-resistance hypothesis (Harvey and Olver 2014, full version, Appendix F) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[HarveyOlver2014](#ref-HarveyOlver2014)]. Here the additional packing representation permits iteration from edge connectivity.

A shortcut matrix is a positive quadratic form that bounds cut sizes; its inverse assigns each edge a resistance, and edges of low resistance are the candidates retained for sparsification. Three steps of the existence argument require new computational work. The shortcut matrix must be consistent with partitions determined by that same matrix; its construction by compactness and a fixed point does not provide a uniform finite search bound. Real whitening may introduce irrational entries, so a polynomial-bit algorithm must specify its numerical data and control every precision loss. Finally, the existential matrix partition theorem of Marcus, Spielman, and Srivastava must be replaced by an algorithm that finds the signs. We use the public constructive method of Ezeunala and Jiang, with a quantitative implementation proved below, while retaining the packing argument.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### A larger cone of geometric tests.

The companion bounds a shortcut matrix on cut vectors. For optimization we use centered positive semidefinite matrices whose squared distances satisfy triangle inequalities. This cone has polynomially many displayed constraints. Enlarging the cone strengthens the required shortcut inequality, so the companion’s cut-only statement cannot simply be applied. We extend its proof by constructing a doubled distance-profile polytope in which radii about vertex images are affine and radial motion has exactly its metric length. The underlying distance-profile isometry is classical (Fréchet 1910; Kuratowski 1935) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Frechet1910](#ref-Frechet1910), [Kuratowski1935](#ref-Kuratowski1935)]; the additional radial identities make the bag and charging arguments work in the new geometry.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Continuous capacities and finite optimization.

Instead of selecting edges abruptly at a resistance threshold, we assign capacities through a continuous ramp. A convex response lowers the resistance cutoff while maintaining the partition inequalities with a controlled loss in packing size. Uncrossing reduces a putative obstruction to a chain of partitions, where the geometric response applies simultaneously. Exact rational separation and a quantitative interior ball turn each response into a finite computation using the ellipsoid method (Grötschel, Lovász, and Schrijver 1988) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GLS1988](#ref-GLS1988)]. Constructive graphic matroid partition then recovers an integer packing (Edmonds 1968; Terao 2023) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Edmonds1968](#ref-Edmonds1968), [Terao2023](#ref-Terao2023)].

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Exact data and certified sparsification.

A log-determinant normalization, following the variational method behind radial isotropic position (Barthe 1998, sec. 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Barthe1998](#ref-Barthe1998)], gives the second vector representation. We approximate the stacked vectors rationally, complete their covariance exactly to the identity, and apply constructive matrix signing. This step builds on the Kadison–Singer partition theorem of Marcus, Spielman, and Srivastava (Marcus, Spielman, and Srivastava 2015) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[MarcusSpielmanSrivastava2015](#ref-MarcusSpielmanSrivastava2015)] and the public rank-one discrepancy method of Ezeunala and Jiang (Ezeunala and Jiang 2026) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[EzeunalaJiang2026](#ref-EzeunalaJiang2026)]; the exact interface and the constant used are proved in Appendix <a href="#app:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:signing">8</a>. Rational matrix certificates imply every cut and packing estimate needed for the next step. The product of the multiplicative losses remains bounded through the iteration. A separate truncation and rounding reduction handles binary multiplicities before any edge expansion: it produces a graph with polynomially many explicit copies, independently of the numerical values of the multiplicities and $k$. The final tree lifts to original edge copies.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Organization.

Section <a href="#tg:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tg:section">2</a> proves the metric-cone hierarchy estimate and its simultaneous response to a partition chain. Section <a href="#sec:ta-algorithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ta-algorithm">3</a> constructs a rational shortcut and a large packing of low-resistance edges. Section <a href="#sec:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:normalization">4</a> builds the rank-preserving frame. Section <a href="#sec:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:signing">5</a> provides exact rational padding and signing certificates. Section <a href="#sec:iteration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:iteration">6</a> combines the two representations, iterates the sparsification, and proves the binary-multiplicity reduction. Section <a href="#sec:fractional-rounding" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fractional-rounding">7</a> records a simultaneous cost-and-cut rounding consequence for rational fractional inputs. Appendix <a href="#app:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:signing">8</a> gives the quantitative rational implementation of the public rank-one signing method.

<a id="tg:section"></a>
### Metric-cone shortcuts for thin trees

We next develop the geometric input to the thin-tree algorithm. The shortcut theorem of the companion (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)] tests a matrix on cuts. Convex optimization in the next section will use a larger, finitely described cone of tests. We establish the corresponding hierarchy estimate by extending its complete geometric arguments, which follow the hierarchy and effective-resistance framework of Anari and Oveis Gharan (Anari and Oveis Gharan 2015, secs. 5–7) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[AnariOveisGharan2015](#ref-AnariOveisGharan2015)].

Throughout this section graphs are finite loopless multigraphs on a vertex set $V$, with $n=|V|\ge2$; edge sets retain multiplicities. All symmetric matrices act on the Euclidean space $\mathsf E=\mathbf 1^\perp\subset\mathbb R^V$. Equivalently, they are centered full matrices, with their zero action on $\mathop{\mathrm{span}}\{\mathbf 1\}$ understood. Write $I_{\mathsf E}$ for the identity on this space, $b_{uv}=e_u-e_v$, and $L_G=\sum_{e\in E(G)}b_eb_e^T$, using arbitrary edge orientations. Matrix pairings are $\ip{A}{Z}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(AZ)$. Define the closed convex cone <a id="tg:test-cone"></a>$$\label{tg:test-cone}
 \mathcal N=\left\{Z\succeq0:
 b_{uw}^TZb_{uw}\le b_{uv}^TZb_{uv}+b_{vw}^TZb_{vw}
 \quad(u,v,w\in V)\right\}.$$ Its trace-dual cone is $\mathcal N^*=\{A:\ip{A}{Z}\ge0\text{ for all }Z\in\mathcal N\}$. The Gram-matrix representation of squared Euclidean distances is classical (Schoenberg 1935) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Schoenberg1935](#ref-Schoenberg1935)]; the additional triangle inequalities give the standard $\ell_2^2$ metric relaxation (Arora, Rao, and Vazirani 2009, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[AroraRaoVazirani2009](#ref-AroraRaoVazirani2009)]. For $Z\in\mathcal N$, the numbers $d(u,v)=b_{uv}^TZb_{uv}$ form a squared Euclidean pseudometric. The centered identity belongs to $\mathcal N$. So does $c_Sc_S^T$, where $c_S=\mathbf 1_S-(|S|/n)\mathbf 1$: its squared distances are precisely the $0$–$1$ cut metric of $S$.

A *hierarchy* is a rooted tree of distinct nonempty subsets of $V$, with root $V$, singleton leaves, and children partitioning their parent into at least two sets. For a nonroot node $A$, with parent $A^*$, set $$P(A)=\delta_G(A),\qquad O(A)=E_G(A,A^*\setminus A).$$ The hierarchy is *locally $h$-connected* if every $G[A]$ is $h$-edge-connected, with the singleton condition vacuous. Each $|O(A)|\ge h$. Each graph edge belongs to exactly two parent boundaries: those of the two children of its endpoints’ least common ancestor. In particular its multiplicity in any selected collection of parent boundaries is at most two.

<a id="stmt-thm-tg-hierarchy"></a>
<div id="thm:tg-hierarchy" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Hierarchy bound for the metric test cone). *There is an absolute integer $h_0$ such that, for every integer $h\ge h_0$, every locally $h$-connected hierarchy of $G$, and every collection $\mathcal M$ of nonroot nodes satisfying $|O(A)|\ge |P(A)|/h$, there is a matrix $D\succ0$ on $\mathsf E$ with <a id="tg:shortcut"></a><a id="tg:hierarchy-resistance"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \ip{D}{Z}&\le\ip{L_G}{Z} &&(Z\in\mathcal N),\label{tg:shortcut}\\
 \frac1{|O(A)|}\sum_{e\in O(A)}b_e^TD^{-1}b_e
 &\le h^{-31/40} &&(A\in\mathcal M).\label{tg:hierarchy-resistance}
\end{aligned}$$ The cutoff is independent of the graph and the hierarchy depth.*

</div>

The proof first expresses the shortcut objective as a ratio whose denominator is total edge length. We then represent its numerator by collections of disjoint balls and charge their radii to edge lengths; the resulting estimate will give simultaneous responses to partition chains.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The conic dual

<a id="stmt-tg-dual"></a>
<div id="tg:dual" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (A geometric upper bound for the shortcut program). *Let $G$ be connected and $\mathcal M$ a nonempty finite collection of nonroot nodes with nonempty parent boundaries. Let $p_*$ be the infimum, over $D\succ0$ satisfying [tg:shortcut](#tg:shortcut), of the largest marked average resistance. Then <a id="tg:dual-ratio"></a>$$\label{tg:dual-ratio}
 p_*\le \sup_{p,U}
 \frac{\displaystyle\sum_{A\in\mathcal M}\frac1{|O(A)|}
       \left(\sum_{e=uv\in O(A)}a_e\right)^2}
      {\displaystyle\sum_{e=uv\in E(G)}d(u,v)},
 \qquad
 a_e=|\ip{u_e}{p_u-p_v}|.$$ Here $p_v$ are Euclidean points whose squared distances $d(u,v)=\norm{p_u-p_v}_2^2$ satisfy the triangle inequalities, the denominator is positive, and $U$ has orthonormal rows $u_e^T$. The Euclidean dimension may be enlarged.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-tg-dual"><summary>Proof of stmt-tg-dual</summary>

*Proof.* Put $A_A=|O(A)|^{-1}\sum_{e\in O(A)}b_eb_e^T$ and $f_A(D)=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(A_AD^{-1})$. These functions are convex, since $$b^TD^{-1}b=\sup_x(2b^Tx-x^TDx).$$ The conic constraint is $L_G-D\in\mathcal N^*$. Choose $D_0=\delta I_{\mathsf E}$ with $0<\delta<\lambda_{\min}(L_G|_{\mathsf E})$. Then $L_G-D_0\succ0$ lies in the interior of $\mathcal N^*$, because the positive semidefinite cone is contained in $\mathcal N^*$. Taking an epigraph height above all $f_A(D_0)$ makes the other inequalities strict as well.

For completeness, finite-dimensional separation gives the needed strong duality even if the infimum is not attained. Separate $(0,0,p_*)$ from the open convex set of $(a,M,z)$ for which some $D\succ0$ and $t\in\mathbb R$ satisfy $$f_A(D)-t<a_A,\qquad
 M-D+L_G\in\operatorname{int}\mathcal N^*,\qquad t<z.$$ Monotonicity in the three coordinates makes the separating coefficients $(\lambda,Z,\gamma)$ satisfy $\lambda\ge0$, $Z\in\mathcal N$, and $\gamma\ge0$. The strictly feasible point just constructed rules out $\gamma=0$: one may take all $a_A<0$ and $M$ in $-\operatorname{int}\mathcal N^*$, making the pairing strictly negative for every nonzero $(\lambda,Z)$. Normalize $\gamma=1$. The unrestricted variable $t$ then forces $\sum_A\lambda_A=1$. Taking limits to the boundary of the open set, and using weak duality for the reverse inequality, gives <a id="tg:lagrange"></a>$$\label{tg:lagrange}
 p_*=\sup_{\substack{\lambda\ge0,\ \sum_A\lambda_A=1\\Z\in\mathcal N}}
 \left[\inf_{D\succ0}
 \{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(A(\lambda)D^{-1})+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(ZD)\}-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(ZL_G)\right],
 \quad A(\lambda)=\sum_A\lambda_AA_A.$$

For any $A,Z\succeq0$, <a id="tg:matrix-infimum"></a>$$\label{tg:matrix-infimum}
 \inf_{D\succ0}\{\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(AD^{-1})+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(ZD)\}
 =2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl((Z^{1/2}AZ^{1/2})^{1/2}\bigr).$$ When $A,Z\succ0$, set $H=Z^{1/2}DZ^{1/2}$ and $C=Z^{1/2}AZ^{1/2}$. The objective minus $2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(C^{1/2})$ is $\norm{H^{-1/2}C^{1/2}-H^{1/2}}_{\mathrm F}^2$, and vanishes at $H=C^{1/2}$. For singular $A$ or $Z$, add $\eta I_{\mathsf E}$ to both. The positive definite identity at each fixed $D$, followed by $\eta\downarrow0$, proves the lower bound. The unperturbed infimum is at most the perturbed infimum, whose limit gives the upper bound. These are auxiliary perturbations for the identity, not extra dual feasibility assumptions.

For $Z\ne0$ in $\mathcal N$, connectivity gives $\mathcal E=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(L_GZ)>0$. Writing $N_0=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}((Z^{1/2}A(\lambda)Z^{1/2})^{1/2})$, optimization over the scale $\tau Z$ gives $$\sup_{\tau\ge0}(2\sqrt\tau N_0-\tau\mathcal E)
 =N_0^2/\mathcal E.$$ The zero multiplier contributes zero. Represent $Z$ as the centered Gram matrix of points $p_v$, and put $$w_e=\sum_{A:e\in O(A)}\frac{\lambda_A}{|O(A)|}.$$ Then $N_0$ is the nuclear norm of the matrix with columns $\sqrt{w_e}(p_u-p_v)$. The singular value decomposition, after adding zero coordinates if needed, gives $$N_0=\max_{UU^T=I}\sum_e\sqrt{w_e}\ip{u_e}{p_u-p_v}.$$ Changing individual row signs preserves row orthonormality, so take the displayed contributions nonnegative. For such a maximizing $U$, $$\begin{aligned}
 N_0
 &\le\sum_A\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_A}{|O(A)|}}
                  \sum_{e\in O(A)}a_e,\\
 N_0^2
 &\le\sum_A\frac1{|O(A)|}
                   \left(\sum_{e\in O(A)}a_e\right)^2.
\end{aligned}$$ The first line uses $\sqrt{\sum_jx_j}\le\sum_j\sqrt{x_j}$ and the second uses $\sum_A\lambda_A=1$. Overlapping boundaries are allowed. Substitution into [tg:lagrange](#tg:lagrange) proves the claim. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### A convex realization with exact radial motion

Starting from the classical distance-profile isometry into $\ell_\infty$ (Fréchet 1910; Kuratowski 1935) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Frechet1910](#ref-Frechet1910), [Kuratowski1935](#ref-Kuratowski1935)], we construct a continuous geometry for an arbitrary finite metric. It will be used for balls and shells; the Euclidean points $p_v$ remain the inputs to the spectral argument. [tg:geometry-figure](#tg:geometry-figure) contrasts the Euclidean spectral points with the distance-profile realization of the same vertex labels and illustrates the exact radial motion of [tg:profiles](#tg:profiles).

<a id="stmt-tg-profiles"></a>
<div id="tg:profiles" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Doubled distance profiles). *Every finite metric $(S,d)$ has an isometric realization $x\mapsto c_x$ in a convex polytope $Q$ in a finite-dimensional normed space with the following properties. Distance $\rho_x(q)=\norm{q-c_x}$ is affine on $Q$. There is a common $R>0$ and a point $c_x^-\in Q$ such that <a id="tg:opposite-radii"></a>$$\label{tg:opposite-radii}
 \norm{q-c_x}+\norm{q-c_x^-}=R\qquad(q\in Q).$$ From any $q\in Q$ one can reach every radial value in $[0,R]$ by moving toward $c_x$ or $c_x^-$, and the change in radius equals the distance moved. Every straight segment in $Q$ has its norm length, and clamped radial functions are piecewise affine on it.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-tg-profiles"><summary>Proof of stmt-tg-profiles</summary>

*Proof.* Take $R>2\max_{x,y}d(x,y)$ and two copies $x^+,x^-$ of each point. On the doubled set define $$\widehat d(x^+,y^+)=\widehat d(x^-,y^-)=d(x,y),\qquad
 \widehat d(x^+,y^-)=R-d(x,y).$$ This is a metric. A triangle with all signs equal is an original triangle. A triangle with two equal signs has two cross-copy sides; an inequality with a cross-copy side on the left reduces to an original triangle inequality. The remaining inequality follows from $d(x,y)+d(x,z)+d(z,y)\le3\operatorname{diam}(d)<2R$.

For a doubled point $a$, let $c_a=(\widehat d(a,z))_z$ be its distance profile in $\ell_\infty$, and set $Q=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{c_a\}$. The triangle inequality and coordinate $a$ show $\norm{c_a-c_b}_\infty=\widehat d(a,b)$. More generally, for $q=\sum_b\theta_bc_b$, <a id="tg:affine-radius"></a>$$\label{tg:affine-radius}
 \norm{q-c_a}_\infty
 =\sum_b\theta_b\widehat d(a,b)=q_a,
 \qquad \theta_b\ge0,\quad\sum_b\theta_b=1.$$ Indeed, convexity of the norm gives the upper bound, and coordinate $a$ gives the lower bound. Thus the radius is the restriction of a linear coordinate function, independently of the representation of $q$. Set $c_x=c_{x^+}$ and $c_x^-=c_{x^-}$. The identity $\widehat d(x^+,b)+\widehat d(x^-,b)=R$ for every doubled point $b$ proves [tg:opposite-radii](#tg:opposite-radii).

If $\rho=\rho_x(q)$, the segment $q_t=(1-t)q+tc_x$ has $\norm{q_t-q}=t\rho$ and $\rho_x(q_t)=(1-t)\rho$. The segment $q_t^-=(1-t)q+tc_x^-$ has $\norm{q_t^--q}=t(R-\rho)$ and $\rho_x(q_t^-)=\rho+t(R-\rho)$. These formulas prove the exact-motion assertion, including the endpoint cases. Convexity of $Q$ keeps all segments in $Q$; the final assertion follows from [tg:affine-radius](#tg:affine-radius) and clamping. ◻

</details>

For a pseudometric, first identify zero-distance points to apply [tg:profiles](#tg:profiles). This identification concerns only the geometric point set. Every graph vertex, edge copy, hierarchy node, and center-vertex label is retained. In particular coincident vertex images do not contract the graph.

<figure id="tg:geometry-figure">

<figcaption>Spectral packing uses Euclidean points; balls and shells use the distance-profile realization with the same vertex labels. The radial paths are schematic: in the ambient norm, distance traveled along either indicated segment equals change of radius. The two segments need not form one straight segment in $Q$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Transferring the bag and charging estimates

The next lemma extends the bag-selection and shell-charging arguments of (OpenAI 2026, Propositions B.7, B.8, and B.10) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)] to $Q$. We retain those constructions and their scalar accounting, but verify the geometric steps below: their stated cube hypotheses do not cover all tests in $\mathcal N$. The two charging alternatives explain the division into compact and assigned bags. Compact bags charge disjoint geometric objects. Assigned bags may overlap geometrically, provided their hierarchy labels separate the graph edges used for charging.

Use relative open balls $B(c,\delta)=\{q\in Q:\norm{q-c}<\delta\}$ and strict shells $B(c;a,b)=\{q\in Q:a<\norm{q-c}<b\}$. An ordinary ball includes its center, whereas a shell with $a=0$ excludes it. Both are called radial objects. An ordinary ball $B(c,b)$ has *numerical* inner radius $a=0$ and width $b$; a shell has width $b-a$. All radial tests and width formulas below apply to both types with these numerical radii, but membership and intersection always use the actual support. In particular, an unchanged ordinary ball keeps its center. A radial restriction to $a\le a'<b'\le b$ means replacement by the strict shell $B(c;a',b')$, even when $a'=0$. Such a replacement is a subset of the old support; it is an operation, not an identification of a ball with a punctured shell. Proposed intervals of nonpositive width are discarded. A *bag* consists of mutually disjoint balls of a common radius $\delta$, centered at vertex images $c_v$. It is *$\beta$-compact of type $(\delta,\Delta)$* if it has at least two balls, its center diameter is at most $\Delta$, and $\beta\Delta\le\delta|\mathrm{Bag}|$. It is *$\eta$-assigned to $A$* if $|\mathrm{Bag}|\ge\eta|O(A)|$ and every ball has a designated center vertex $u\in A$ with an edge $uv\in O(A)$ of length $d(u,v)<\delta$. A family has all its balls mutually disjoint and a common radius. A compact family has a common type $(\delta,\Delta)$. An assigned family has one bag for each of a set of distinct assigned nodes. Bags assigned to the same node at one scale may always be merged: this preserves every witnessing edge, the assignment bound, and the total mass. Fix $0<\rho<1$. A sequence of compact families is $\rho$-geometric when $\Delta_{i+1}<\rho\delta_i$; a sequence of assigned families is $\rho$-geometric when $\delta_{i+1}<\rho\delta_i$ and its assigned nodes are distinct across families. In either case its mass is $$\mathsf M=\sum_i\delta_i\sum_{\mathrm{Bag}\in\mathcal F_i}
                                      |\mathrm{Bag}|.$$

<a id="stmt-tg-transfer-assigned"></a>
<div id="tg:transfer-assigned" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Geometric transfer). *<span id="tg:transfer" label="tg:transfer"></span> Let $p_v\in\mathbb R^{d_{\mathrm E}}$ be points for which $d(u,v)=\norm{p_u-p_v}_2^2$ is a pseudometric. Realize $d$ in $Q$ as in [tg:profiles](#tg:profiles). Let $U:\mathbb R^{d_{\mathrm E}}\to\mathbb R^{E(G)}$ be a contraction, change its row signs so $a_e=\ip{u_e}{p_u-p_v}\ge0$, and set $$d_e=d(u,v),\qquad
 N_A=\frac{(\sum_{e\in O(A)}a_e)^2}{|O(A)|},\qquad
 \mathcal E=\sum_{e\in E(G)}d_e.$$ The following three assertions hold in $Q$.*

1.  *<span id="tg:transfer-extract" label="tg:transfer-extract"></span> Let $T$ be a nonempty set of nodes with $N_A>0$ and $N_A\ge\alpha\sum_{e\in O(A)}d_e$, where $0<\alpha\le1$. Define $$\begin{aligned}
     J&=1+\lceil\log_2(256/\alpha^2)\rceil,&
     C_2&=128J^4,&\sigma&=\alpha/C_2,\\
     L&=2+\lceil\log_2(1/\sigma)\rceil,&
     H_0&=1+\lceil\log_2(24/(\rho\sigma^2))\rceil,&
     C_1&=1024\epsilon^{-2}.
    \end{aligned}$$ If $\beta>1$, $0<\epsilon<1/3$, $0<\rho<1$, and $\sigma^\epsilon\le(48\beta C_1)^{-1}$, there is a $\rho$-geometric sequence consisting either of $\beta$-compact families or of $\sigma^{1+2\epsilon}$-assigned families, with <a id="tg:captured-mass"></a>$$\label{tg:captured-mass}
     \mathsf M\ge
     \frac{\sigma^\epsilon}{384\beta C_1C_2LH_0}
                      \sum_{A\in T}N_A.$$*

2.  *<span id="tg:transfer-compact" label="tg:transfer-compact"></span> For $G$ $k$-edge-connected, a $\rho$-geometric sequence of $\beta$-compact families with $\rho\le1/12$ and $\beta\ge36$ satisfies $\mathcal E\ge(k/4)\mathsf M$.*

3.  *For a locally $k$-connected hierarchy, selected nodes satisfying $|O(A)|\ge k\rho|P(A)|$, and a $\rho$-geometric sequence of $(24C_3/k)$-assigned families, one has <a id="tg:assigned-charge"></a>$$\label{tg:assigned-charge}
     \mathcal E\ge\frac{kC_4}{96C_3}\mathsf M$$ provided $C_4\ge3$, $\rho\le(6C_4)^{-1}$, and $C_3\ge2((C_4+1)+4(C_4+2)^2)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-tg-transfer-assigned"><summary>Proof of stmt-tg-transfer-assigned</summary>

*Proof.* We follow the constructions in Sections B.2–B.4 of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)], keeping their scale choices and scalar potentials. All balls, shells, and intersections now lie in the continuous polytope $Q$. New shells inherit vertex centers, so [tg:affine-radius](#tg:affine-radius) remains available. We first obtain the bags, then explain how each of the two charging constructions operates in this realization.

*Extraction and the spectral step.* The two truncations and homogeneity arguments in (OpenAI 2026, sec. B.2.2) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)] use only $a_e^2\le d_e$, nonnegative boundary sums, and multiplicity at most two. They select nonempty subsets $O'(A)\subseteq O(A)$ retaining a fixed fraction of each squared boundary sum, and group the nodes so that both positive quantities $a_e^2$ and $d_e$ vary by a factor less than four within $F=\bigcup_AO'(A)$. For one such group put $N=\sum_A|O(A)|$, $N'=|F|$, $c_1=\min_{e\in F}a_e^2$, and $c_2=\max_{e\in F}d_e$. The truncation and grouping estimates give $c_1N'\ge\sigma Nc_2$. For the Euclidean points $Y_v=Up_v$ and $\widetilde\alpha=\sigma N/N'$ this implies $$(\mathbb E_{e\in F}a_e)^2\ge c_1
 \ge\widetilde\alpha c_2
 \ge\widetilde\alpha\mathbb E_{e\in F}\norm{Y_u-Y_v}_2^2.$$ This is the exact hypothesis of the Euclidean spectral-packing Lemma B.3 of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)]; that lemma places no binary restriction on its points. Its selected centers have squared Euclidean separation at least $4r$. Since <a id="tg:spectral-to-metric"></a>$$\label{tg:spectral-to-metric}
 \norm{Y_u-Y_v}_2^2\le d(u,v)=\norm{c_u-c_v}_\infty,$$ the corresponding $Q$-balls of radius $r$ are disjoint. If $b_0$ is their number, the spectral lemma gives $b_0r\ge\widetilde\alpha^\epsilon c_1N'/C_1$. Shrink their common radius to $\delta$ defined by $b_0\delta=\widetilde\alpha^\epsilon c_1N'/C_1$. Only the selected vertex labels pass from the Euclidean geometry to $Q$; a map on intermediate points is unnecessary.

The rest of the one-family construction uses a maximal disjoint collection of balls of radius $D=\max\{\delta,2c_2\}$, centered at images $c_u$ for which $u\in A$ and $uv\in O'(A)$ for some $A$. Maximality puts every candidate center within $2D$ of a selected center: intersection of two relative balls implies this distance bound by the triangle inequality. Every endpoint center is within $c_2$ of an assigned endpoint, hence within $3D$ of a selected center. Grouping by those centers gives diameter at most $6D$ in the compact case. In the assigned case the witnessing edge has length at most $c_2<D$. The cardinality estimates of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma B.6) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)] give compactness, assignment strength, and radius range $[\sigma c_1,12c_1/\sigma]$. Its final grouping by dyadic size and then by residue class modulo $H_0$ is numerical: each scale lies between $\sigma2^j$ and $24\,2^j/\sigma$, so scales in the same residue class meet the stated geometric separation. The losses $C_2$, $L$, $2H_0$, and $192\beta C_1/\sigma^\epsilon$ are exactly those in [tg:captured-mass](#tg:captured-mass). This proves (i).

*Length charging and compact radial objects.* The resource charged by both arguments is one fixed straight segment $[c_u,c_v]$ of length $d_e$ for each graph edge, retaining parallel edges as separate resources. For a radial object with numerical radii $a<b$, its clamped radial function is $f(q)=\min\{b,\max\{a,\norm{q-c}\}\}$. It is $1$-Lipschitz and piecewise affine on each edge segment. If the object has vertex witnesses at radius at most $a$ and at least $b$, all its intermediate radial cuts are nontrivial. Consequently $$k(b-a)\le\int_a^b|\delta_G\{v:\norm{c_v-c}<t\}|\,dt
 =\sum_{uv\in E(G)}|f(c_u)-f(c_v)|.$$ For disjoint radial objects, subdivide an edge segment at the finitely many breakpoints of these functions. At most one clamped function is nonconstant on each open subsegment, and its variation is bounded by that subsegment’s length. The total charge is therefore at most $d_e$, including edges along a boundary or of zero length. This proves the length bound of (OpenAI 2026, Lemma B.9) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)] in $Q$. For an ordinary ball, nonconstant portions of the clamped function lie at strictly positive radius. Its center contributes no charged length, but remains part of the ball in membership tests.

For compact bags, process the families in decreasing order of radius, as in (OpenAI 2026, Proposition B.8) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[ThinTreesCompanion](#ref-ThinTreesCompanion)]. An input ball inserted at the current scale is called fresh and is left intact until the next scale; all other live objects are called old. Thus a fresh ordinary ball becomes an old ordinary ball at the next scale unless a subsequent operation restricts it. Disjoint equal-radius balls have centers separated by at least $2\delta$, since their midpoint belongs to $Q$. For any live object with center $c$ and numerical radii $a,b$, call $B(c_u,\delta)$ interior when $$a+\delta+\Delta\le\norm{c_u-c}\le b-\delta-\Delta.$$ This conservative two-sided test also applies when the object is an ordinary ball, with $a=0$. If one ball of a compact bag is interior, its containing object is old, since fresh balls and unprocessed balls belong to the same disjoint family. All bag centers have radial values in an interval $[l,u]\subseteq[a+\delta,b-\delta]$ of width at most $\Delta$.
